<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>16:T500,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">private</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> Object</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">[] elements;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">private</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> int</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> size;</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">public</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> Object </span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">pop</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() { </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">return</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> elements[</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">--</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">size]; }</span></span></code></pre>17:T451,<p>The <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Garbage collection" data-def="The JVM automatically finding objects that are no longer reachable and reclaiming their memory.">garbage collector</button> decides when an unreachable object is processed, and the finalizer thread may run at low priority — so a finalizer might run seconds later, much later or never before the JVM exits.</p>
<p>Resources released only by finalizers can run out long before then, like file descriptors.</p>
<p>Objects with finalizers also take much longer to allocate and reclaim, and an exception thrown inside one is swallowed silently.</p>
<p>Deterministic cleanup belongs in <code>close()</code>, called from <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="try-with-resources" data-def="&lt;code&gt;try (var in = open()) { … }&lt;/code&gt; — closes every resource declared in the parentheses automatically, in reverse order, even when an exception is thrown. Requires &lt;code&gt;AutoCloseable&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">try-with-resources</button>.</p>
18:T4ae,<p>With try-finally, if the body throws and then <code>close()</code> in the <code>finally</code> block throws too, the second exception propagates and the first is discarded.</p>
<p>The first one usually explains the problem, like a read error, while the second is a consequence of it.</p>
<p><button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="try-with-resources" data-def="&lt;code&gt;try (var in = open()) { … }&lt;/code&gt; — closes every resource declared in the parentheses automatically, in reverse order, even when an exception is thrown. Requires &lt;code&gt;AutoCloseable&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">try-with-resources</button> keeps the original exception and attaches the <code>close</code> failure to it as a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Suppressed exception" data-def="An exception thrown while closing a resource after another exception already happened. &lt;code&gt;try&lt;/code&gt;-with-resources keeps the first and attaches the others, retrievable with &lt;code&gt;getSuppressed()&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">suppressed exception</button>, retrievable with <code>getSuppressed()</code>.</p>
19:T711,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">BufferedReader br </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> BufferedReader</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> FileReader</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(path));</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">try</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">    return</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> br.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">readLine</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">} </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">finally</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">    br.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">close</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>1a:T8b3,<p>This <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="try-with-resources" data-def="&lt;code&gt;try (var in = open()) { … }&lt;/code&gt; — closes every resource declared in the parentheses automatically, in reverse order, even when an exception is thrown. Requires &lt;code&gt;AutoCloseable&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">try-with-resources</button> opens two streams. While copying, <code>transferTo</code> throws an exception — call it <strong>A</strong>. Closing a stream can throw too — call that <strong>B</strong>. Put what happens next in order.</p>
<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">try</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> (</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">var</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> in </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> FileInputStream</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(src);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">     var</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> out </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> FileOutputStream</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(dst)) {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">    in.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">transferTo</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(out);   </span><span style="--shiki-light:#6A737D;--shiki-dark:#6A737D">// throws A</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>1b:T662,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">CaseInsensitiveString cis </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> CaseInsensitiveString</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF">"Polish"</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">String s </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF"> "polish"</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">cis.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">equals</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(s); </span><span style="--shiki-light:#6A737D;--shiki-dark:#6A737D">// true</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">s.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">equals</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(cis); </span><span style="--shiki-light:#6A737D;--shiki-dark:#6A737D">// ?</span></span></code></pre>1c:T6c9,<p>If <code>ColorPoint.equals</code> checks colour, then <code>point.equals(colorPoint)</code> is true but <code>colorPoint.equals(point)</code> is false — <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Symmetric" data-def="If &lt;code&gt;x.equals(y)&lt;/code&gt; then &lt;code&gt;y.equals(x)&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">symmetry</button> breaks.</p>
<p>If it ignores colour when comparing with a plain <code>Point</code>, then red and blue points can both equal the same plain point, breaking <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Transitive" data-def="If &lt;code&gt;x&lt;/code&gt; equals &lt;code&gt;y&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;y&lt;/code&gt; equals &lt;code&gt;z&lt;/code&gt;, then &lt;code&gt;x&lt;/code&gt; equals &lt;code&gt;z&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">transitivity</button>.</p>
<p>There&#39;s no way to satisfy both while also keeping the <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Liskov substitution principle" data-def="Anything true of a type should be true of its subtypes — a subclass instance must work wherever the superclass is expected." data-book="Effective Java">Liskov substitution principle</button>, so favour composition: <code>ColorPoint</code> holds a <code>Point</code> and offers <code>asPoint()</code>.</p>
<p>Records and <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Value class" data-def="A class whose instances represent a value, like &lt;code&gt;Integer&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;LocalDate&lt;/code&gt;, where two instances with the same contents should be equal." data-book="Effective Java">value classes</button> sidestep the issue because they&#39;re final.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">p.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">setName</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF">"Grace"</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">    (o1, o2) </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">-></span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> o1.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">hashCode</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">-</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> o2.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">hashCode</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">();</span></span></code></pre>21:T5d8,<p>The constructor stores references to the same <code>Date</code> objects the caller still holds.</p>
<p><code>private final</code> stops <code>Period</code> from reassigning those fields, but the objects they point to are mutable, so <code>end.setTime(0)</code> changes the period&#39;s end from the outside.</p>
<p>The cure is a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Defensive copy" data-def="Copying a mutable argument when it comes in, or a mutable field when it goes out, so callers can&amp;#39;t change your object&amp;#39;s internals behind your back." data-book="Effective Java">defensive copy</button> in the constructor (and in <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Accessor method" data-def="A method that returns the value of a field — the public way in, instead of making the field itself public." data-book="Effective Java">accessors</button>), or using an <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Immutable" data-def="An object whose state can&amp;#39;t change after construction. Simple, thread-safe, freely shareable. Make fields &lt;code&gt;final&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;private&lt;/code&gt;, provide no setters, and don&amp;#39;t let the class be extended." data-book="Effective Java">immutable</button> type like <code>Instant</code> so there&#39;s nothing to copy.</p>
<p>This is why immutability must hold for the whole object graph a class exposes, not just its own fields.</p>
22:T98a,<pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light github-dark" style="--shiki-light:#24292e;--shiki-dark:#e1e4e8;--shiki-light-bg:#fff;--shiki-dark-bg:#24292e" tabindex="0"><code><span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">Date start </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Date</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">0</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">Date end </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Date</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">1000</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">Period p </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> new</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Period</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(start, end);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">end.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">setTime</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">0</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">System.out.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">println</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(p.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">end</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">().</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">getTime</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">());</span></span></code></pre>23:T40b,<p><code>HashSet.addAll</code> happens to be implemented by calling <code>add</code> for each element — a fact documented nowhere in its contract.</p>
<p>The subclass counts 3 in its <code>addAll</code> override, then calls <code>super.addAll</code>, which calls the overridden <code>add</code> three more times, each adding 1 again.</p>
<p>The bug comes from depending on the superclass&#39;s <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Self-use" data-def="A class calling its own overridable methods. Subclasses that override them can be surprised, so it must be documented." data-book="Effective Java">self-use</button>, which can change in any release; a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Wrapper class" data-def="A class that wraps another object of the same interface and adds behavior — the Decorator pattern." data-book="Effective Java">wrapper class</button> that forwards to a private <code>Set</code> never sees these internal calls and counts exactly once.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">final</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> class</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Sub</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> extends</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> Super</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> {</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">    Sub</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() { </span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">super</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(); instant </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> Instant.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">now</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(); }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">    @</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">Override</span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583"> void</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0"> overrideMe</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">() { System.out.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">println</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(instant); }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#6A737D;--shiki-dark:#6A737D">// main: new Sub();</span></span></code></pre>27:T415,<p>Implementing an interface announces to the world that your class <em>is</em> that type — clients can assign it to the interface and rely on that forever.</p>
<p>A constant interface makes using some constants a permanent part of the public API, even if a later version no longer needs them.</p>
<p>The constants also leak into every subclass&#39;s namespace. Put constants in the class they belong to, an enum, or a <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Noninstantiable class" data-def="A class of only static members, like &lt;code&gt;Math&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;Collections&lt;/code&gt;. Gets a private constructor so no one can create an instance." data-book="Effective Java">utility class</button>, and use <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Static import" data-def="&lt;code&gt;import static java.lang.Math.PI;&lt;/code&gt; — lets you use a class&amp;#39;s static members without the class name." data-book="Effective Java">static imports</button> for brevity.</p>

<p>An identity function doesn&#39;t depend on the type at all, so one shared instance can be handed out for every <code>T</code> with an <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Unchecked warning" data-def="The compiler saying it can&amp;#39;t verify type safety at this point. Eliminate them, or prove safety and suppress with &lt;code&gt;@SuppressWarnings(&amp;quot;unchecked&amp;quot;)&lt;/code&gt; on the smallest scope." data-book="Effective Java">unchecked cast</button> that&#39;s provably safe.</p>
<p>The generic factory method hides that cast and gives each caller the type it asked for — avoiding a new object per type.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">f.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">putFavorite</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(Integer.class, </span><span style="--shiki-light:#005CC5;--shiki-dark:#79B8FF">0xcafebabe</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">String s </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> f.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">getFavorite</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(String.class);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">int</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> i </span><span style="--shiki-light:#D73A49;--shiki-dark:#F97583">=</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8"> f.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">getFavorite</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(Integer.class);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">System.out.</span><span style="--shiki-light:#6F42C1;--shiki-dark:#B392F0">printf</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">(</span><span style="--shiki-light:#032F62;--shiki-dark:#9ECBFF">"%s %x%n"</span><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">, s, i);</span></span></code></pre>2f:T420,<p>An <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Unchecked warning" data-def="The compiler saying it can&amp;#39;t verify type safety at this point. Eliminate them, or prove safety and suppress with &lt;code&gt;@SuppressWarnings(&amp;quot;unchecked&amp;quot;)&lt;/code&gt; on the smallest scope." data-book="Effective Java">unchecked cast</button> <code>(T) obj</code> is <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Erasure" data-def="Generic type arguments are checked at compile time and then removed, so at run time a &lt;code&gt;List&amp;lt;String&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; is just a &lt;code&gt;List&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">erased</button> to nothing at run time, so a wrong value would slip out and fail later.</p>
<p><code>Class.cast</code> actually checks that the object is an instance of the class and throws immediately if not.</p>
<p>Since the <code>Class&lt;T&gt;</code> key carries the real type, the container can guarantee every value it returns matches what was asked for.</p>

Which one runs is decided at compile time from the static types." data-book="Effective Java">Overloading</button> is resolved at compile time from the declared types of the arguments.</p>
<p>In the loop, the variable <code>c</code> is declared as <code>Collection&lt;?&gt;</code>, so the compiler chooses <code>classify(Collection&lt;?&gt;)</code> for every call — the run-time classes <code>HashSet</code> and <code>ArrayList</code> never matter.</p>
<p><button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Overriding" data-def="A subclass redefining an inherited method. Which one runs is decided at run time from the object&amp;#39;s actual class." data-book="Effective Java">Overriding</button> is the opposite: the method runs based on the object&#39;s run-time class.</p>
<p>Code that needs run-time dispatch should use a single method with <code>instanceof</code> checks or a pattern-matching switch.</p>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#6A737D;--shiki-dark:#6A737D">    // if (size == 0) throw new EmptyStackException();</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="--shiki-light:#24292E;--shiki-dark:#E1E4E8">}</span></span></code></pre>46:T426,<p>With a serialization proxy, the serialized form is a simple private nested class holding just the logical state.</p>
<p>On <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Deserialization" data-def="Turning a byte stream back into objects. Acts as a hidden constructor that attackers can feed any bytes to." data-book="Effective Java">deserialization</button>, its <code>readResolve</code> rebuilds the real object through the public constructor or factory, so every invariant check and <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Defensive copy" data-def="Copying a mutable argument when it comes in, or a mutable field when it goes out, so callers can&amp;#39;t change your object&amp;#39;s internals behind your back." data-book="Effective Java">defensive copy</button> runs as usual.</p>
<p>No instance of the real class is ever created from raw bytes, and its fields can stay <code>final</code>.</p>
<p>Forged streams aimed at the real class are rejected, because its <code>readObject</code> simply throws.</p>

It can&#39;t check the element type, which was <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Erasure" data-def="Generic type arguments are checked at compile time and then removed, so at run time a &lt;code&gt;List&amp;lt;String&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; is just a &lt;code&gt;List&lt;/code&gt;." data-book="Effective Java">erased</button>, so it succeeds with an <button type="button" class="term" aria-expanded="false" data-term="Unchecked warning" data-def="The compiler saying it can&amp;#39;t verify type safety at this point. <p><code>l</code> now claims to be a <code>List&lt;String&gt;</code> but holds <code>Integer</code>s.</p>
<p>The first time code reads an element as a <code>String</code>, the compiler&#39;s hidden cast fails with <code>ClassCastException</code>, far from the faulty cast.</p>
